# Requirement-to-test inventory

**Behavioral authority:** `documentation/specification/2026-10-21 - specification handoff.md`, consolidated 2026-10-21, at commit `7870042a02e094dca772d6fdd6570ad7b2d02578`.
**Organizational authority:** `documentation/specification/2026-10-21 - test suite structure.md`, v2, same commit.
**Provenance:** user-approved six-wave plan. Wave 1 was expressly authorized; the forwarded OK for its latest correction authorizes Wave 2 only. No subsequent Architect decision is used.
**Review status:** Wave 1 accepted by the forwarded independent OK for `7364ed8843c565592d4bfa550d90c218988c83ba`, verified on remote main before Wave 2. Wave 2 is submitted for its own independent review; it is not accepted. Waves 3–6 remain planned/unimplemented.

## Status meanings and evidence limits

- **Implemented / integration unavailable:** real executable assertions exist at the exact node IDs below. All 632 conversion cases currently fail with `ImplementationUnavailable` because no verified production implementation/API exists. No transformation assertion has passed or been certified by this run.
- **Harness only / passed:** 49 independent fixture/RDF-helper checks passed. They are listed separately and receive no DirectKG transformation coverage credit.
- **Planned:** later-wave assertions are not implemented merely because they appear here.
- **Blocked for clarification:** the affected exact expectation has no normative answer; independent assertions continue. No skip/xfail hides this status.

The observed run collected 681 cases: 49 passed, 632 failed at the integration boundary, no skips/xfails or collection errors. No other failure class was observed. See `tests/README.md` for reproducible commands and binding boundaries. This is assertion traceability, not a line-coverage metric or a claim of exhaustive inputs.

Rule IDs in each implemented test's `Rules:` docstring match its inventory references. Exact sections supplement IDs for unnumbered clauses. Each parameter row is explicit below. Runtime mutations start from the complete listed source and are written to pytest temporary files; these are reviewed case edits, not simplified replacement export schemas or a fixture-builder framework. No generated runtime output belongs in fixture directories.

Earlier Wave 1 self-check correction (`12f7906`): generic attribute missing-name diagnostics (DK-IRI-04)
and the integrated extensionality INFO (DK-DIAG-17) check identification of the
affected declaration through any unambiguous actually reported locator. They do
not require a literal source ID where the rule does not mandate one. The binding
records this identification separately from explicitly reported IDs; class-fallback
and duplicate-ID assertions retain their explicit-ID requirements. The ordinary
ID-use case also rejects forbidden missing-name events independently of locator
format. Node IDs, fixture pairings, and approved wave scope are unchanged.

Wave 1 reviewer correction (F1–F3): identifier destinations are established
without reading the identifier under test; reused files are promoted to canonical
shared bundles with every consumer/pairing updated; and a two-declaration input
with IDs `"C17"` and `" C17 "` exercises exact comparison without trimming under
all three input policies. Existing node IDs are preserved, including harness
IDs for promoted inputs. Only the four new cases listed below are added.

## Fixture provenance and contract checks

All stored source models are purpose-authored to the inspected **ontouml-vp-plugin 0.5.3** structure, not claimed to be literal exports from a running VP installation. The official release resolves to commit `9fc02b8162db9910f21b8713549cafce2627dad0`:
<https://github.com/OntoUML/ontouml-vp-plugin/tree/9fc02b8162db9910f21b8713549cafce2627dad0>.

Inspected files under `src/main/java/it/unibz/inf/ontouml/vp/model/` establish the used shapes:

| Used structure | 0.5.3 evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Project/model root, nesting, identifiers | `vp2ontouml/IProjectTransformer.java`; `ontouml/serialization/ProjectSerializer.java`, `PackageSerializer.java`, `ElementSerializer.java`, `ModelElementSerializer.java` |
| Classifier/property fields, explicit false/nulls, reference objects | `ontouml/serialization/ClassSerializer.java`, `ClassifierSerializer.java`, `RelationSerializer.java`, `PropertySerializer.java`, `Serializer.java`, `DecoratableSerializer.java` |
| Plain text and bounded localized carrier interpretation | `ontouml/serialization/MultilingualTextSerializer.java`; RD-27 in the normative baseline. No newer document structure is used by these fixtures. |
| Custom tag value shapes and dedicated-field separation | `vp2ontouml/ITaggedValueTransformer.java`; custom strings/numbers/booleans are used. Built-in `isExtensional` is supplied as its dedicated class field, not as a custom tag. |
| Diagram owner/contents, class/relation views, rectangles, routed paths | `ontouml/serialization/DiagramSerializer.java`, `DiagramElementSerializer.java`, `NodeViewSerializer.java`, `ClassViewSerializer.java`, `ConnectorViewSerializer.java`, `RelationViewSerializer.java`, `ShapeSerializer.java`, `RectangularShapeSerializer.java`, `PathSerializer.java` |
| Explicit generalization reference fields | `ontouml/serialization/GeneralizationSerializer.java` and actual Catalog examples |

The Catalog files `OntoUML/ontouml-models/models/library/ontology.json` (blob `0ef9f0b0543eb31035489907160d9759c6166d45`) and `models/guizzardi2005ontological/ontology.json` (blob `8d48eac24e8bb451893ec136be1bb2f366edb28e`) corroborated nested Package contents, complete Class/Property/Relation variants, null fields, and `{id,type}` references. Their top-level data did **not** declare an exporter version. No raw Catalog file was copied into this suite or certified as a 0.5.3 fixture.

There are 29 stored JSON sources: 19 contract-positive cases (including the explicit no-validation-of-omitted-view-IDs case), 9 parseable governed negatives (2 unknown-information cases, 7 duplicate-declaration cases), and 1 intentionally malformed JSON document. The exact-ID-comparison positive reuses the inspected complete Project/Package/Class fields and has two category classes with IDs `"C17"` and `" C17 "`; it introduces no new exporter structure. Fixture checks verify the named authoring defects, reference/type resolution, binary ends, and the presentation structures used here. They are bounded authoring checks, not a production validator or a compatibility claim for arbitrary schema documents.

The 5 stored Turtle files are **partial ground asserted graphs**. Their exact input/expectation pairing is recorded below, including cross-bundle use of the canonical shared core. Unknown concrete DKGM identity, optional metadata, and unspecified generated-comment wording prevent a whole-output fixture from being normative. Inter-conversion comparisons select fixed domain predicates, retain required source labels/IDs, and permit the stated unordered pairwise disjointness presentation. Serialization round trips and access within the same conversion use whole-graph isomorphism. None of these checks requires an OWL reasoner, textual Turtle order, stable blank-node labels or byte-identical output.

Equivalent cases are grouped deliberately: the complete stereotype table is tested with default tolerant input handling, with selected cells repeated under all input policies to establish precedence. Every-policy repetition is retained for explicit policy-independent fatal/nonfatal guarantees. Ordinary collision suffix allocation is checked as a complete suffix set and repeated source mapping; ID ordering is not invented where the rule leaves it open.

Wave 2 self-check correction after `0307dd346c91346fa38bda2f8d57c9930b00751f`:
the existing literal-fallback matrix now supplies `{"en":"___","pt-BR":"東京"}`,
whose supported localized values yield no usable naming candidate. Six new
locations cover both strategies and every input policy, checking own-ID fallback,
diagnostic conditions, both original language-tagged labels, complete membership,
closure, and inequality. All previous 675 node IDs remain. This corrects missing
coverage of DK-IRI-10's empty eligible-candidate branch; it does not change scope
or accepted transformation behavior. Independent Wave 2 review remains pending.


Wave 2 adds six purpose-authored JSON sources and three partial ground Turtle
expectations (34 total stored JSON/Turtle files). Literal and GeneralizationSet
shapes were checked directly against the same 0.5.3 commit:

| Additional structure | Inspected source and blob evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Literal: base model-element fields only | `ontouml/serialization/LiteralSerializer.java`, blob `4256ad9b7a9300121ec517e08173f93d3f3c7106` |
| GS: boolean flags, nullable categorizer ref, generalization ref array | `ontouml/serialization/GeneralizationSetSerializer.java`, blob `7127f412b19233be1f2f1fd054e354c821b8faae` |
| Explicit general/specific reference objects | `ontouml/serialization/GeneralizationSerializer.java`, blob `121d9d0eb7436e0a14b28bddff798cb567ca1c96` |
| Nullable class fields; order serialized as string | `ontouml/serialization/ClassSerializer.java`, blob `10f408517e16428d840a264e3dfe801f033d2722` |
| Valid nature names used by restrictedTo | `ontouml/model/Nature.java`, blob `f872a64fc0fc822309c3a0b7b253b16635ecfa91` |
| Supported languageString mapping and top-level Project.languages | Official `OntoUML/ontouml-schema`, `src/ontouml-schema.yaml`, blob `68d20679b0b553b0ce81a18a3bab49ef50b18ea0`; only RD-27 carriers are used, never its newer root/elements document layout |
| Actual ontouml-js carrier implementations | Official `OntoUML/ontouml-js` development `src/language_string.ts`, blob `dc7fb9e19c8316edd9d9dc41056a02f11d296b44` (`toJSON` maps original language keys to text); `src/project.ts`, blob `599e31ce036b5c0d8b3d07d0287c034d0cb34c04` (`languages` serialized as array). Its other metadata/element layout is not imported |

Runtime negative mutations retain the full exporter structure and introduce only
the stated defect. Independent harness checks cover invalid literal IDs/lists,
unresolved refs, and source traversal. Audit-only malformed language/categorizer
cases do not assert production recovery where the specification leaves it open.
OWL2 permits `owl:members` and `owl:distinctMembers` on AllDifferent; the helper
accepts both, arbitrary collection order, equivalent anonymous enumeration
expressions, and multiple groups establishing all required distinct pairs.
See the primary [OWL2 RDF mapping](https://www.w3.org/TR/owl2-mapping-to-rdf/).
It rejects missing membership or improper closure without simulating a converter.

Wave 2 deliberately groups equivalent cases: one hierarchy-free stereotype
matrix covers every required/forbidden pair; member counts and flags isolate
coverage/disjointness branches; all input policies are repeated for explicit
fatal/nonfatal guarantees. The language precedence matrix is shared across
source names/descriptions; representative class/attribute/value paths exercise
each selector boundary, while all 16 supported entity/end text paths exercise
placement and lexical preservation. This avoids a redundant product of every
stereotype, text field, language context, and policy. Flag-comment wording is
unfixed, so actual RDF comments are read through test-side semantic observations,
with their exact subject/literal verified, rather than an invented message template.

## Blockers, exclusions, and placement reconciliation

- **Blocked for Architect clarification:** DK-GEN-04's concrete stable `U` is
  unspecified. The existing vocabulary URI is not substituted for an accepted
  decision. Import-shape assertions do not certify its exact identity or contents.
- **Integration unavailable:** no production API/CLI exists in the inspected
  repository. All 632 transformation nodes fail clearly at that boundary.
  No skip/xfail, fake transformer, reasoner entailment or supplied expected graph
  substitutes for execution. A real invocation binding, including audited reads
  of actual comment/diagnostic meanings, remains necessary.
- **Assigned Wave 2 bounds:** no behavior is invented for duplicate GS members,
  missing/malformed membership containers, or partial categorizer recovery.
  Higher-order recursion is MAY; full instantiation non-reconciliation remains
  the already-planned Wave 5 interaction. Null isAbstract treatment is not
  asserted; absence is distinct from explicit exported boolean false.
- The enum-owned-property warning clarification is SHOULD. Recommended Unicode
  normalization mechanics, preferred suffix-allocation method, exact message
  text/codes, optional recursion and example-only RDF presentation are not made
  mandatory. The qualitative avoidance of repetitive language warnings has no
  specified numerical limit; tests accept consolidated messages and do not
  invent a count. No claim is made for a diagnostic-text language representation
  absent from the public interface; fixed English RDF comments and source-ID
  exclusions have concrete assertions.
- Future-wave exact-name blockers for unusable inverse roles/domain qualifiers
  remain unchanged. No additional exporter/schema support, exceptional escaped-
  ID collision disposition, future instantiation links or unspecified runtime
  failures are inferred.
- No path exceptions. Wave 2 creates `test_classes.py`, `test_higher_order.py`,
  `test_labels.py`; extends `test_ontology.py` and the existing fixture harness;
  extends only prescribed support; revises README and this single inventory.
  Its actual data is four `classes/` input bundles (with set/enum expectations),
  one `higher_order/categorizers/` input/expectation bundle, and canonical
  `shared/source_text/input.json`. Source text is shared from its first use
  because ontology and labels topics both consume it. Existing shared base/core/
  property-collision bundles and all Wave 1 node IDs remain intact. No unused
  labels fixture directory, replacement register, test config or builder is added.
- The structure document's canonical relocation remains deferred under the
  explicit prohibition on specification edits. No production code, vocabulary,
  either governing specification or unrelated application/configuration is edited.
- **Reviewer gate:** Wave 2 is submitted, not accepted. Its published artifacts
  and exact SHA require an independent verdict. Wave 3 must not start until the
  user forwards an applicable OK for the latest submitted Wave 2 revision.
